## Audit of the related diffusion task

I inspected the full Codex task **Run a small diffusion model** and checked its local artifacts under `/Users/yash/Documents/Resume/video-diffusion-lab`.

### Wan2.1 on the Mac: not completed

- The task created `run_pilot.py` for Wan2.1 T2V 1.3B using Apple's MLX implementation, with intended 9-frame, 832×480, 8-step output and per-step latent files.
- The run was interrupted while downloading model files. The source repo lists 16.37 GiB of weights; the local Hub cache was only about 192 MiB. No Wan frames, latents, or manifest were produced under that pilot's `run/` folder.
- This was not the target LatentGate Diffusers/T4 run.

### CogVideoX-2B on the free Colab T4: completed feasibility pilot

- The earlier Colab notebook is [CogVideoX-2B pilot](https://colab.research.google.com/drive/12x0MtizsWW5QcUivqQTVu3ji-BM9zPEb). It ran `zai-org/CogVideoX-2b` with FP16 and sequential CPU offload on a Tesla T4.
- The final run requested 13 frames at the model's 720×480 resolution and 50 denoising steps. The pipeline returned 16 frames and the task saved 11 latent checkpoints at steps 1, 5, 10, …, 50. The manifest records seed 2026, guidance scale 6.0, and 798.63 seconds of generation time.
- The [final contact sheet](/Users/yash/Documents/Resume/video-diffusion-lab/run/cogvideox_2b_chunk13/final_contact_sheet.png) shows a visible red ball, but it drifts left and upward despite a rightward-motion prompt. A pixel-threshold check in that task measured the ball centroid moving from x≈449.7 to x≈414.1 and y≈247.3 to y≈225.4 across the sequence.
- The two earlier 480×320 runs were outside the model's documented 720×480 resolution. The 8-step output was nearly white; the 30-step output is diagnostic only. The final 720×480 run is the useful feasibility evidence.
- Source outputs: [manifest](/Users/yash/Documents/Resume/video-diffusion-lab/run/cogvideox_2b_chunk13/manifest.json), [frames and latent checkpoints ZIP](/Users/yash/Documents/Resume/video-diffusion-lab/run/cogvideox_2b_chunk13.zip), and [pilot notes](/Users/yash/Documents/Resume/video-diffusion-lab/README.md).

This proves the free T4 can run a short video diffusion model with CPU offload and preserve intermediate sequence latents. It does not establish LatentGate's hypotheses or count as M0: it is CogVideoX, the frames are denoised jointly, and the manifest lacks a pinned model commit, full package versions, VAE round-trip measurements, predicted-clean previews, and transformer/attention captures.

### Updated first-control candidate from the sibling research task

The active research task has narrowed its first intervention to **DiTraj-style 2D trajectory control** of one object under a fixed camera, with apparent size and scene quality as guardrails. The current local notebook specifies six matched cases (mirrored left/right paths × Base / foreground-background separation / full DiTraj) using a direction-neutral prompt, shared start box, and common initial noise. It runs a one-step smoke first and keeps the 30-step screen disabled by default. The saved M0 clip was scored as an image-space baseline: 17/17 detections, normalized mean center error 0.148, mean target-box IoU 0.186, all rightward center steps, 7.21% mask-area CV, and +25.96% endpoint mask-area change. The red mask is only a quick secondary screen; the blinded per-frame annotation reliability check remains open. **No DiTraj video generation, paired policy run, or steering result exists yet.** This is a trajectory-control pilot, not a 3D geometry, contact, or physics claim.

The DiTraj paper/code now rank ahead of the earlier VIGOR reward-smoke proposal for the first intervention because per-frame boxes directly specify the motion target. The upstream implementation uses a different pinned stack (Diffusers 0.33.1, PyTorch 2.7.1, Transformers 4.52.4) and defaults to BF16/81 frames; T4 use requires a separately measured FP16 route or an explicit port. Its README states CC BY-NC 4.0, so keep the adaptation in the research-only track unless the terms are clarified. Preserve [`research/2026-10-02_vigor_research_memo.md`](research/2026-10-02_vigor_research_memo.md) as a secondary geometry-reward audit: VIGOR's static-world reprojection score can penalize valid object motion, while Frame Guidance remains a later selected-frame intervention candidate and GEM-4D a longer-term reference.

One research claim needs careful wording: the [VIGOR repository](https://github.com/Ethan2k04/VIGOR) says code, dataset, and pretrained LoRA adapters were released, but the public repo page has no GitHub release asset and the linked author Hugging Face profile lists no public model. Treat the adapter as **claimed but not located/verified**, not conclusively unavailable. The public GB3DV dataset card reports a generic `cc` license label; review dataset and VGGT checkpoint terms before reuse or redistribution.

## Compute preflight

Colab was opened in the requested Chrome profile and a new notebook, `LatentGate M0 - Runtime Probe`, was created.

Notebook: [LatentGate M0 - Runtime Probe](https://colab.research.google.com/drive/1jmYmwpV70RonpQUuQV-nbEcJ0Bzby1wI)

Observed in a diagnostic cell:

- Runtime: Google Compute Engine GPU backend, Tesla T4
- GPU memory reported by PyTorch: 14.56 GiB
- Python: 3.13.15
- PyTorch: 2.11.0+cu128
- CUDA runtime: 12.8
- `torch.cuda.is_available()`: `True`

The T4 option was selectable and allocated successfully. Premium GPU options were disabled in the runtime selector at that time. Colab availability and limits can change, so record the assigned device with each run.

The upstream model card estimates 8.19 GB VRAM for the 1.3B model and recommends 480p. That suggests the T4 is suitable for a baseline, but instrumentation and generation settings can raise peak use; measure rather than assume.

**Precision adjustment for this GPU:** NVIDIA lists the T4 at compute capability 7.5; its CUDA compute-capability table shows FP16 Tensor Core support but no BF16 support at 7.5. Use FP16 for this T4 run instead of copying the model card's BF16 example. Revisit precision if Colab assigns an Ampere-or-newer GPU.

That runtime probe preceded model loading. The later retry4 run did load the pinned Wan Diffusers checkpoint, generate the baseline, and complete its replay; its remaining evidence gaps are recorded below.

## Current M0 implementation target

- Use `Wan-AI/Wan2.1-T2V-1.3B-Diffusers` at immutable revision `0fad780a534b6463e45facd96134c9f345acfa5b`, returned by the official Hugging Face model API on 2026-10-01.
- The allocated Colab T4 runtime recorded Python 3.13.15, PyTorch 2.11.0+cu128, CUDA 12.8, Diffusers 0.40.0, Transformers 5.16.1, Accelerate 1.14.0, Hugging Face Hub 1.29.0, SentencePiece 0.2.2, and Safetensors 0.8.0. These are recorded in [the run config](m0/run_config.yaml) and the run manifest.
- Use FP16 on the T4 and keep the VAE in FP32. NVIDIA identifies T4 as compute capability 7.5, which supports FP16 Tensor Core operations but not BF16 Tensor Core operations.
- Diffusers' Wan callback runs after `scheduler.step`, so it captures the next latent state. For this pinned UniPC run, predicted-clean states are captured from the scheduler's exact `convert_model_output()` result before the scheduler updates the sample.
- Capture a small set of transformer-block summaries on selected steps. Full video self-attention matrices are too large to materialize; selected attention summaries need an explicitly bounded/chunked implementation.

### Retry5: complete microscope run

The Colab notebook [LatentGate M0 - Wan Microscope](https://colab.research.google.com/drive/1jmYmwpV70RonpQUuQV-nbEcJ0Bzby1wI) is exported at [`m0/notebooks/LatentGate_M0_Wan_Microscope.ipynb`](m0/notebooks/LatentGate_M0_Wan_Microscope.ipynb). It uses the pinned Wan2.1 T2V 1.3B revision, prompt, seed 2026, UniPC scheduler, 832×480 resolution, 17 frames, and 30 denoising steps. The manifest's M0 assessment is `complete`, with all ten gates true.

- Runtime: Colab Tesla T4, Python 3.13.15, PyTorch 2.11.0+cu128, CUDA 12.8, Diffusers 0.40.0. Transformer effective compute is FP16; the VAE is FP32. Peak allocated GPU memory was 7.91 GiB.
- Dtype detail: transformer inputs and reported effective compute are FP16, but transformer parameter storage is mixed: 1,415,869,504 FP16 elements and 3,127,296 FP32 elements. The first-parameter-only FP32 finding from retry4 was a false positive, not a model-load failure.
- Time: 160.02 seconds for baseline generation and 142.10 seconds for the exact same-seed replay. These figures exclude setup and model downloads.
- Captures: post-step latents at steps 0, 4, 9, 14, 19, 24, and 29; predicted-clean latents and decoded previews at steps 4, 14, 24, and 29; hidden-state summaries 18/18; bounded attention summaries 18/18.
- Predicted-clean tensor checks: each `x0_hat` has shape `[1, 16, 5, 60, 104]`, is finite, and records the UniPC `convert_model_output` path for `predict_x0=true` and `prediction_type=flow_prediction`, along with its step, timestep, sigma, input sample, and guided velocity.
- Replay: initial noise, final latent, and all seven saved post-step latent checkpoints are bitwise identical across same-seed replay. Retry5's 17 final frame PNGs and seven saved post-step latent tensors also match retry4 byte-for-byte / exactly.
- Artifact integrity: all 128 paths in the manifest's SHA-256 map exist and match; the Generation Packet hash matches the manifest, the 127 pre-packet hashes match, and ZIP integrity passes. The 129-file artifact directory includes the manifest, which does not hash itself.
- VAE round trip: status `ok`; MAE 0.001013, RMSE 0.001918, PSNR 54.34 dB, maximum absolute pixel error 0.1892. The averages are not a per-pixel error guarantee.

The [autopsy report](m0/runs/wan_t4_2026-10-02_retry5/autopsy_report.md), [Generation Packet](m0/runs/wan_t4_2026-10-02_retry5/generation_packet.json), and [final frame contact sheet](m0/runs/wan_t4_2026-10-02_retry5/run_001/frames_contact_sheet.png) summarize the run. ### Retry4: historical run with a capture gap

[`m0/runs/wan_t4_2026-10-02_retry4`](m0/runs/wan_t4_2026-10-02_retry4) is the first instrumented baseline and exact replay. Architecture, post-step latents, hidden summaries, bounded-attention summaries, VAE round trip, and replay passed. Predicted-clean latents/previews were missing because the initial runner supported the FlowMatch Euler formula but the pinned pipeline uses `UniPCMultistepScheduler`. Retry5 added capture through UniPC's own flow-prediction conversion. Retry4's original config snapshot and incomplete manifest are retained as historical records.

The dtype failure in the retry4 report was a measurement bug, not a loading failure. Wan intentionally keeps `rope`, `time_embedder`, `scale_shift_table`, and selected normalization modules in FP32. The corrected runner reports effective transformer compute separately from the full parameter dtype profile ([Wan model source](https://github.com/huggingface/diffusers/blob/v0.40.0/src/diffusers/models/transformers/transformer_wan.py#L2534), [dtype helper](https://github.com/huggingface/diffusers/blob/v0.40.0/src/diffusers/models/modeling_utils.py#L2455)).

### Post-hoc frame observation

The prompt asks for a bright red ball to roll right across a fixed-camera tabletop. The final frames show rightward motion. A color-mask proxy places the ball centroid at x=209.01 in frame 0 and x=241.57 in frame 16 (+32.56 px), with +6.26 px vertical drift. Its thresholded bounding box grows from 120×127 to 133×144 px; mask area grows 25.96%. Because retry5's final frames are byte-for-byte identical to retry4, this audit applies to both runs. The apparent size/shape growth is a candidate consistency issue, not ground-truth segmentation or metric 3D evidence. Method and per-frame measurements are in [`wan_t4_retry5_frame_motion_audit.json`](m0/analysis/wan_t4_retry5_frame_motion_audit.json).

### Loading history

The first eager pipeline load hit the Colab host-RAM ceiling while loading UMT5. A separate, memory-capped UMT5 pass successfully precomputed both prompt embeddings; an `AutoTokenizer` attempt failed because the Diffusers repository has no root `config.json`, and an `Identity` text-encoder placeholder was rejected by Diffusers. The successful run used the repository's concrete `T5Tokenizer` and passed `text_encoder=None` while supplying both precomputed embedding tensors. The low-memory route completed sampling and replay.

### Current research update (2026-10-02)

- The first feedback hypothesis is a one-time, confidence-gated signed residual update to DiTraj's later foreground cross-attention boxes after zero-based step 4. Keep the first-three-step hard RoPE geometry fixed. This is a candidate control action, not a claim that it moves the rendered object monotonically.
- Pinned DiTraj source inspection found the cross-attention mask is constructed before the denoising loop, each step creates a fresh attention dictionary, and the callback runs after `scheduler.step`. The adapter therefore needs persistent bbox state and per-step mask rebuilding. A CPU smoke against the actual pinned Diffusers 0.33.1 UniPC scheduler captured step 4 with max absolute difference 0.0 from its flow-prediction conversion. Full DiTraj pipeline integration, T4 capture, and in-loop VAE cost remain unmeasured. See [`ditraj_feedback_adapter_design_v1.md`](research/ditraj_feedback_adapter_design_v1.md), [`smoke_pinned_unipc_capture.py`](research/smoke_pinned_unipc_capture.py), and [`ditraj_unipc_capture_smoke_v1.json`](research/ditraj_unipc_capture_smoke_v1.json).
- The corrected red-object observer check on 34 Kubric RGB frames against segmentation masks gives 34/34 scoreable frames and 1.73 px mean center error (0.00927 frame diagonals). The five controller frames have 0.0243 mean L2 continuous control-grid error after first-frame anchoring; that is not discrete token error. Raw absolute first-frame center error averages 1.76 px and must pass independently because anchoring hides it. The in-sample 0.65 heuristic accepts 33/34 frames on these same two correlated development paths; it is not held-out calibration. This supports observer mechanics on synthetic red spheres only, not Wan output or policy gain. See [`ditraj_red_observer_kubric_calibration_v1.json`](research/ditraj_red_observer_kubric_calibration_v1.json) and [`evaluate_ditraj_red_observer_kubric.py`](research/evaluate_ditraj_red_observer_kubric.py).
- The local M0 predicted-clean observer screen only compares decoded previews to the final frames from the same seed/prompt using the same red proxy. It is not independent observer accuracy or policy gain. Two human labels for the Kubric annotation reliability packages remain pending.
- Local source notebook: [`ditraj_paired_path_pilot_v1.ipynb`](research/ditraj_paired_path_pilot_v1.ipynb). Remote Kaggle draft still has only setup, blank, and duplicate T4-preflight cells; runner/results are absent. No GPU run or paid compute occurred. Colab free-GPU allocation remains usage-limited.

### 2026-10-02 feedback-continuation update

- A measurement review caught and corrected the red-observer pixel metric. Current aggregate is 34/34 scoreable, 1.7297 px mean center error (0.009270 frame diagonals), 1.8672 px p95, and 1.7613 px mean raw frame-0 error. At the ten controller keyframes, first-frame-anchored trajectory error averages 0.024335 L2 continuous units on the 52×30 control grid; these are not quantized token errors. The 0.65 detector stability cutoff accepts 33/34 on the same two correlated Kubric paths and remains in-sample only. Regression coverage now tests anisotropic pixel scaling and the separate keyframe/start metrics.
- An independent code audit found and fixed the feedback runner's annotation-template argument mismatch. The staged runner now writes a gate-failure report and exits unsuccessfully unless the decode-matched no-op is frame-identical to baseline and live feedback changes at least one rasterized attention token. This only gates mechanism plumbing; no Wan/DiTraj GPU generation has run.
- Updated the design to require a held-out generated-preview observer gate with blinded frame labels before efficacy, and to compare six arms: open-loop, exact no-op, frozen static correction, live residual, shuffled residual, and sign-reversed residual. Efficacy needs at least ten matched seed blocks before statistical claims; five is only a directional screen.

### 2026-10-11 Wan temporal sampling audit

- Static CPU audit of the saved Wan2.1 M0 packet: 17 decoded RGB frames map to a latent tensor of shape `[1,16,5,60,104]`; VAE metadata reports temporal compression 4 and spatial compression 8. The five latent-time positions' nominal frame anchors `[0,4,8,12,16]` match the planned DiTraj anchor list. The anchors are stride references, not disjoint frame groups or exact latent receptive fields.
- Recommendation: keep framewise RGB measurements for visibility, identity, boxes, and trajectory; index internal-state readouts by latent-time position and denoising step. Do not require chunked generation absent a measured memory, latency, or control-horizon constraint. This audit loaded no model, used no GPU, and did not compare chunked inference. See [`m0_temporal_sampling_audit_v1.json`](research/m0_temporal_sampling_audit_v1.json) and [`audit_m0_temporal_sampling.py`](research/audit_m0_temporal_sampling.py).

### 2026-10-11 five-point temporal readout probe

- Compared the 17-frame red-mask trace with piecewise-linear interpolation from nominal latent-aligned anchors `[0,4,8,12,16]`. At the registered threshold, omitted-frame center error averages 0.8599 px (RMSE 0.9274 px; max 1.6929 px at frame 3), and mask-area relative error averages 0.5409% (max 1.2155% at frame 10). Recomputed the mask over the existing 4×3×3 threshold grid: all 36 cases retained full coverage; mean center error ranged 0.7189–0.9468 px, per-case maximum center error 1.5029–2.0168 px, and mean mask-area relative error 0.4363–1.5957%. The proxy x-center is strictly increasing across all 16 RGB intervals.
- A schedule-only enumeration shows the five anchors intersect 5/17 possible one-frame event windows (29.4%), 8/16 two-frame windows (50.0%), 11/15 three-frame windows (73.3%), and every four-frame window. These are uniform-start-position coverage counts, not observed event rates. So five points preserve this clip's smooth proxy path but can miss brief events; keep all RGB frames for event/visibility/identity evaluation. This is a same-sample proxy check, not observer validation or a general sampling claim. No model, new segmentation, GPU, or chunked inference was used. See [`m0_temporal_downsampling_probe_v1.json`](research/m0_temporal_downsampling_probe_v1.json) and [`probe_m0_five_point_temporal_readout.py`](research/probe_m0_five_point_temporal_readout.py).

- Read the full three-turn [Video Diffusion Roadmap chat](https://chatgpt.com/share/6abe4e3d-4570-83ea-8e63-5f0c3962fb9e) and reconciled its broad objective with the active repository plan. It is not a Wan implementation: it uses a separate 12-layer/768-dim DiT, SDXL VAE image latents, action tokens, and DDPM noise schedules. The paper reports 100K-step DROID pretraining on four A100 GPUs for 24 hours. Its official code/checkpoint repository does not expose a software license, so no code or weights were run or reused. SUAVE remains a direct discrete unified-video/action prior-art comparator; VPDD is a separate-video/action precedent. ### 2026-10-16 16:24 CST M1 CogVideoX temporal support and bundle repair

- A source-only audit of the exact `zai-org/CogVideoX-5b-I2V` revision `a6f0f4858a8395e7429d82493864ce92bf73af11` with Diffusers 0.40.0 derives the VAE timeline as 49 RGB output frames → 13 temporal latent positions → 49 decoded frames. Two temporal downsample stages preserve index 0 and pool adjacent pairs after it; nominal pooling ancestry is frame 0, then frames 1–4, 5–8, …, 45–48. Causal convolutions broaden context, so these are not exclusive RGB receptive fields.
- The VAE's kernel-3 causal convolutions replicate the first feature twice on the left edge and add no right temporal pad. Both compression inputs (49 and 25) are odd, so all trailing positions pair without dropping frame 48. The pinned base I2V transformer has no `patch_size_t`; no patch padding or crop applies. I2V conditioning uses one encoded input-image latent plus 12 zero conditioning latents, separately from the 13-position denoising sample.
- For nominal sparse RGB inspections `[0,4,8,…,48]`, schedule-only enumeration misses 36/49 possible one-frame windows (73.5%), 24/48 two-frame windows (50.0%), 12/47 three-frame windows (25.5%), and 0/46 four-frame windows. ### 2026-10-17 08:31 CST M2 descriptor-based ACL query source review

- Apple's `acl_get_fd_np(fd, type)` API and public Libc header confirm that Darwin can read `ACL_TYPE_EXTENDED` ACLs directly from an open descriptor; the header also defines the ACL type, entry-iteration, and allow/deny constants. Replaced the helper's absolute-path `/bin/ls -lde` checks with fail-closed `ctypes` bindings to `acl_get_fd_np`, `acl_get_entry`, `acl_get_tag_type`, and `acl_free`. ACL checks now use the retained/opened directory or file descriptor; listed files are opened with `O_NOFOLLOW` and checked against the prior no-follow stat identity.
- The helper remains **unverified**. Apple documents the native calls, but this turn did not execute, compile, or test the helper. In particular, empty-ACL `EINVAL` handling, ctypes ABI/symbol resolution, and target-filesystem behavior require an isolated fixture review. A null result, unsupported ACL filesystem, unexpected errno/tag, missing symbol, or failed free fails closed. Descriptor-based ACL access removes the path-query race but does not lock out same-UID concurrent mutation; use only a quiescent owner-only window after separate isolated validation.
- Evidence and decision are in [the custody helper audit memo](research/2026-10-17_gate1_custody_preflight_helper_audit.md), with primary references to Apple's [`acl_get_fd_np`](https://developer.apple.com/library/archive/documentation/System/Conceptual/ManPages_iPhoneOS/man3/acl_get_fd_np.3.html), Apple Libc [`sys/acl.h`](https://github.com/apple-oss-distributions/Libc/blob/main/include/sys/acl.h), Apple [`acl_get_entry`](https://developer.apple.com/library/archive/documentation/System/Conceptual/ManPages_iPhoneOS/man3/acl_get_entry.3.html), and Python [`ctypes`](https://docs.python.org/3.13/library/ctypes.html). Helper SHA-256 `1de329c0e6313b1f71a026bd6b10b930ccc73e7be65f41eac3805b063b86752b`; memo SHA-256 `c811cea8e5573958f6584554f49e2eb87cffe1d8f289d212931086f379f40abc`.
- Gate 1 is still blocked on two independent complete v5 human returns; scan v16 shows zero complete/partial returns. No private custody data, scanner/scorer, tests, runtime, or compute was used.
